r1

=== BUGGY CODE ===
import math
from typing import List
import bisect

class Solution:
    def maximumSumQueries(self, nums1: List[int], nums2: List[int], queries: List[List[int]]) -> List[int]:
        def buildSparseTable(arr, n):
            k = int(math.log2(n))
            st = [[None for j in range(n)] for i in range(k + 1)]
            for i in range(k + 1):
                for j in range(n - (1 << i) + 1):
                    if i == 0:
                        st[i][j] = arr[j]
                    else:
                        st[i][j] = max(st[i - 1][j], st[i - 1][j + (1 << (i - 1))])
            return st

        def getMax(st, l, r):
            i = int(math.log2(r - l + 1))
            return max(st[i][l], st[i][r - (1 << i) + 1])

        n = len(nums1)
        a = sorted([[nums1[i], nums2[i]] for i in range(n)])
        max_y = [v[1] for v in a]
        for i in range(n - 2, -1, -1):
            max_y[i] = max(max_y[i], max_y[1 + 1])   
        _sum = [v[0] + v[1] for v in a]
        st_max_sum = buildSparseTable(_sum, n)
        res = []
        for x, y in queries:
            i = bisect.bisect_left(a, [x, y])
            if i == n or max_y[i] < y:
                res.append(-1)
                continue
            left, right = i, n - 1
            while left < right:
                m = (left + right + 1) // 2
                if max_y[m] >= y:
                    left = m
                else:
                    right = m - 1
            j = right
            res.append(getMax(st_max_sum, i, j))
        return res

=== PROMPT ===
Observe the following faulty python code. It contains one or more bugs.
Fix the code and output only the fixed code.

```python
import math
from typing import List
import bisect

class Solution:
    def maximumSumQueries(self, nums1: List[int], nums2: List[int], queries: List[List[int]]) -> List[int]:
        def buildSparseTable(arr, n):
            k = int(math.log2(n))
            st = [[None for j in range(n)] for i in range(k + 1)]
            for i in range(k + 1):
                for j in range(n - (1 << i) + 1):
                    if i == 0:
                        st[i][j] = arr[j]
                    else:
                        st[i][j] = max(st[i - 1][j], st[i - 1][j + (1 << (i - 1))])
            return st

        def getMax(st, l, r):
            i = int(math.log2(r - l + 1))
            return max(st[i][l], st[i][r - (1 << i) + 1])

        n = len(nums1)
        a = sorted([[nums1[i], nums2[i]] for i in range(n)])
        max_y = [v[1] for v in a]
        for i in range(n - 2, -1, -1):
            max_y[i] = max(max_y[i], max_y[1 + 1])   
        _sum = [v[0] + v[1] for v in a]
        st_max_sum = buildSparseTable(_sum, n)
        res = []
        for x, y in queries:
            i = bisect.bisect_left(a, [x, y])
            if i == n or max_y[i] < y:
                res.append(-1)
                continue
            left, right = i, n - 1
            while left < right:
                m = (left + right + 1) // 2
                if max_y[m] >= y:
                    left = m
                else:
                    right = m - 1
            j = right
            res.append(getMax(st_max_sum, i, j))
        return res
```

Reason in a repair-and-recheck way.

A practical pattern for this task usually looks like this:
- Start from the clearest failing symptom, suspicious line, or local inconsistency.
- Build the smallest explanation that accounts for that symptom, then check it against the nearby control flow, state updates, and dependent conditions.
- Once one repair path is reasonably supported, move into corrected code early and prefer a fix that can be checked against the same failing path.
- After the edit, rerun that path or the most relevant edge case.
- Then do one small coupled-region scan: a directly connected guard, index update, return path, or call site that could still break the same fix.
- If the recheck is still off, refine the same local region before opening a distant new theory.
- Once the failing path and its directly coupled region look consistent, finalize the code.

Let's define a sorted by nums1. Query x,y. i = bisect_left(a, [x,y]) as above. Is it always true that for all k >= i, nums1[k] >= x? Let's prove: If there is any pair with nums1==x and nums2>=y, i is first such pair, so all k>=i have nums1>=x. If no such pair, i is first pair with nums1>x, so all k>=i have nums1>x. If there are pairs with nums1==x and nums2<y, they are before i, not included. So yes.

Now, max_y[i] < y means no valid pair in suffix i..n-1? max_y[i] is max nums2 in suffix. If max_y[i] < y, all nums2 < y, none valid. If >=, there is at least one valid.

Then binary search rightmost j such that max_y[j] >= y. Because max_y non-increasing, for all k <= j, max_y[k] >= y, so each suffix k..n-1 contains at least one valid. For all k > j, max_y[k] < y, no valid in suffix k..n-1. Thus valid indices are not necessarily contiguous, but every valid index l must satisfy l <= j? Wait if l is valid, then max_y[l] >= nums2[l] >= y, so l <= j by rightmost. So all valid indices are in [i, j]. Also for any k in [i,j], suffix k contains at least one valid, but k itself may be invalid. The maximum sum over valid indices in [i,j] equals maximum over all indices in [i,j]? Need prove for any invalid k in [i,j], there exists valid l >= k with sum >= sum_k. Since max_y[k]>=y, choose l >= k with nums2[l]>=y. Then nums1[l] >= nums1[k] because sorted. So sum_l = nums1[l]+nums2[l] >= nums1[k]+y > nums1[k]+nums2[k] = sum_k because nums2[k] < y. Thus invalid k cannot be the unique max; for every invalid k, there is a valid l with strictly greater sum. Therefore max over [i,j] equals max over valid indices. Great. So algorithm correct.

Now, maybe there is a bug in the code's `max_y[i] = max(max_y[i], max_y[1 + 1])`; if n=2, out of range. If n=3, for i=1, max_y[1] = max(max_y[1], max_y[2]) okay; for i=0, max_y[0] = max(max_y[0], max_y[2]) not suffix. So bug.

Now, let's think about if there is a bug in the binary search's initial `right = n - 1`. If n=0? no.

For range length 6 l=0,r=5 uses i=2, st[2][0] covers [0,3], st[2][2] covers [2,5]. st[2][2] built? j range 0..2, yes. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a bug in the sparse table's `_sum` calculation: `_sum = [v[0] + v[1] for v in a]`. If nums1 and nums2 can be negative, sum can be negative. Sparse table max handles negative. But constraints likely positive. If all sums negative, getMax returns negative. Good.

Potential issue: If no valid pair, return -1. If valid pair sum can be -1? If nums can be negative, sum might be -1, but problem likely positive. If sum could be -1 and valid, returning -1 would be ambiguous. But constraints positive. Not issue.

Now, maybe there is a bug in the code's `max_y[i] = max(max_y[i], max_y[1 + 1])`; if n=2, out of range. If n=3, for i=1, max_y[1] = max(max_y[1], max_y[2]) okay; for i=0, max_y[0] = max(max_y[0], max_y[2]) not suffix. So bug.

Now, let's think about if there is a bug in the binary search's initial `right = n - 1`. If n=0? no.

Potential issue: The code's `getMax` function uses `max(st[i][l], st[i][r - (1 << i) + 1])`. If `l` and `r - (1<<i)+1` are the same, max same. Good.

Now, maybe there is a bug in `buildSparseTable` if `n` is not power of two and `k = int(math.log2(n))`; for n=6, k=2. st[2] built for j=0,1,2? n-4+1=3. getMax for range length 5 l=0,r=4 uses i=2, st[2][0] covers [0,3], st[2][1] covers [1,4]. valid. For range length 6 l=0,r=5 uses i=2, st[2][0] covers [0,3], st[2][2] covers [2,5]. st[2][2] built? j range 0..2, yes. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a bug in the algorithm if there are duplicate pairs. Sorted list includes duplicates. bisect_left works. Sparse table max handles duplicates. Suffix max handles. Good.

Let's check for i=2, j max n-4. Need st[1][j+2] valid. Row 1 built for j range n-2+1 = n-1, so indices 0..n-2. j+2 <= n-2? Since j <= n-4, j+2 <= n-2. valid. For i=k, j max n-2^k. Actually row k-1 built for j in range(n - 2^(k-1) + 1), so max index n - 2^(k-1). j+2^(k-1) <= n - 2^k + 2^(k-1) = n - 2^(k-1). valid. Good.

Now, maybe there is a bug in the code's `max_y[i] = max(max_y[i], max_y[1 + 1])`; if n=2, out of range. If n=1, loop empty. So fix.

Potential issue: If no valid pair, return -1. If valid pair sum can be -1? If nums can be negative, sum might be -1, but problem likely positive. If sum could be -1 and valid, returning -1 would be ambiguous. But constraints positive. Not issue.

With this update, yes. But if we initialize `max_y = [v[1] for v in a]`, then for i from n-2 down to 0, `max_y[i] = max(max_y[i], max_y[i+1])`. This sets suffix max. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a bug in `getMax` if `r - (1 << i) + 1` could be negative? No because 2^i <= length, so r - 2^i + 1 >= l >=0.

With this update, yes. But if we initialize `max_y = [v[1] for v in a]`, then for i from n-2 down to 0, `max_y[i] = max(max_y[i], max_y[i+1])`. This sets suffix max. Good.

Actually row k-1 built for j in range(n - 2^(k-1) + 1), so max index n - 2^(k-1). j+2^(k-1) <= n - 2^k + 2^(k-1) = n - 2^(k-1). valid. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a bug in `getMax` if `r - (1 << i) + 1` could be negative? No because 2^i <= length, so r - 2^i + 1 >= l >=0.

Now, maybe the original code has a bug in `max_y` update line but also in the binary search: It uses `max_y[m] >= y` to find rightmost index with at least one valid in suffix. But if max_y is non-increasing, true set is prefix. However, if max_y has values [5,4,5] due bug, not non-increasing. Fix max_y.

Now, let's think about if there is a bug in the code's `max_y` suffix max if we fix to `max_y[i] = max(max_y[i], max_y[i + 1])`. But note we also need to ensure `max_y` is non-increasing. With this update, yes. But if we initialize `max_y = [v[1] for v in a]`, then for i from n-2 down to 0, `max_y[i] = max(max_y[i], max_y[i+1])`. This sets suffix max. Good.

Now, maybe there is a bug in the code's `max_y[i] = max(max_y[i], max_y[1 + 1])`; if n=2, out of range. If n=1, loop empty. So fix.

Need st[k-1][j+2^(k-1)] valid. Row k-1 built for indices 0.. n - 2^(k-1) - 1? Actually row k-1 built for j in range(n - 2^(k-1) + 1), so max index n - 2^(k-1). j+2^(k-1) <= n - 2^k + 2^(k-1) = n - 2^(k-1). valid. Good.

=== RAW ANSWER ===
```python
import math
from typing import List
import bisect

class Solution:
    def maximumSumQueries(self, nums1: List[int], nums2: List[int], queries: List[List[int]]) -> List[int]:
        def buildSparseTable(arr, n):
            k = int(math.log2(n))
            st = [[None for _ in range(n)] for _ in range(k + 1)]
            for i in range(k + 1):
                for j in range(n - (1 << i) + 1):
                    if i == 0:
                        st[i][j] = arr[j]
                    else:
                        st[i][j] = max(st[i - 1][j], st[i - 1][j + (1 << (i - 1))])
            return st

        def getMax(st, l, r):
            i = int(math.log2(r - l + 1))
            return max(st[i][l], st[i][r - (1 << i) + 1])

        n = len(nums1)
        a = sorted([[nums1[i], nums2[i]] for i in range(n)])

        max_y = [v[1] for v in a]
        for i in range(n - 2, -1, -1):
            max_y[i] = max(max_y[i], max_y[i + 1])

        _sum = [v[0] + v[1] for v in a]
        st_max_sum = buildSparseTable(_sum, n)

        res = []
        for x, y in queries:
            i = bisect.bisect_left(a, [x, y])
            if i == n or max_y[i] < y:
                res.append(-1)
                continue

            left, right = i, n - 1
            while left < right:
                m = (left + right + 1) // 2
                if max_y[m] >= y:
                    left = m
                else:
                    right = m - 1

            j = right
            res.append(getMax(st_max_sum, i, j))

        return res
```

=== FIXED CODE ===
import math
from typing import List
import bisect

class Solution:
    def maximumSumQueries(self, nums1: List[int], nums2: List[int], queries: List[List[int]]) -> List[int]:
        def buildSparseTable(arr, n):
            k = int(math.log2(n))
            st = [[None for _ in range(n)] for _ in range(k + 1)]
            for i in range(k + 1):
                for j in range(n - (1 << i) + 1):
                    if i == 0:
                        st[i][j] = arr[j]
                    else:
                        st[i][j] = max(st[i - 1][j], st[i - 1][j + (1 << (i - 1))])
            return st

        def getMax(st, l, r):
            i = int(math.log2(r - l + 1))
            return max(st[i][l], st[i][r - (1 << i) + 1])

        n = len(nums1)
        a = sorted([[nums1[i], nums2[i]] for i in range(n)])

        max_y = [v[1] for v in a]
        for i in range(n - 2, -1, -1):
            max_y[i] = max(max_y[i], max_y[i + 1])

        _sum = [v[0] + v[1] for v in a]
        st_max_sum = buildSparseTable(_sum, n)

        res = []
        for x, y in queries:
            i = bisect.bisect_left(a, [x, y])
            if i == n or max_y[i] < y:
                res.append(-1)
                continue

            left, right = i, n - 1
            while left < right:
                m = (left + right + 1) // 2
                if max_y[m] >= y:
                    left = m
                else:
                    right = m - 1

            j = right
            res.append(getMax(st_max_sum, i, j))

        return res
